Study aid · Practice MCQs (written for this guide; answers cite the source card)
Q1. Hotel tariff ₹2,500 + ₹450 GST; Level 9 ceiling ₹2,812.50. Reimbursement per O.M. 29.06.2018 (as illustrated)?
  1. ₹2,812.50
  2. ₹2,500
  3. ₹2,950
  4. ₹3,318.75

Answer: (c) ₹2,950 — Tariff within ceiling → rent + full GST = ₹2,950. Rule citation: ch07-010 (L1674–1700)

Q2. Under O.M. 01.02.2018, if both boarding and lodging are free:
  1. 25% flat DA is paid
  2. Hotel and Food components are NIL; local travel under Para 2E(i)/(iii) remains admissible
  3. Full DA is paid
  4. Only food is paid

Answer: (b) Hotel and Food components are NIL; local travel under Para 2E(i)/(iii) remains admissible — The 25% flat DA is discontinued. Rule citation: ch07-008 (L1616–1642)

Q3. O.M. 22.12.2020 extends the no-voucher/self-certification route for local travel to:
  1. Levels 12 and above
  2. Levels 9–11
  3. Level 5 and below only
  4. All levels

Answer: (b) Levels 9–11 — “removes the receipt/voucher condition for Pay Levels 9–11”. Rule citation: ch07-012 (L1720–1739)

Q4. Retirement CTG after O.M. 06.01.2022, settling at the last station with actual change of residence?
  1. One-third
  2. Nil
  3. Full 80% of last month’s basic pay
  4. 100%

Answer: (c) Full 80% of last month’s basic pay — 20-km restriction abolished; full 80% if actual change of residence; else nil. Rule citation: ch07-013 (L1740–1761)

Q5. How many Authorized Travel Agents are specified for Government-funded air travel (31.12.2021)?
  1. One
  2. Two
  3. Three
  4. Five

Answer: (c) Three — Balmer Lawrie, Ashok Travels & Tours, IRCTC. Rule citation: ch07-017 (L1842–1866)

Q6. Per the guide, who pays joining time pay under Rule 7, CCS (Joining Time) Rules?
  1. Old office
  2. New administrative authority
  3. Shared equally
  4. Government servant

Answer: (b) New administrative authority — “paid for strictly by the new administrative authority”. Rule citation: ch07-019 (L1902–1941)

Chapter 8 — Dearness Allowance Crosses 50% · source lines 1952–2059

Chapter 8 — Targeted Dearness Allowance Update

Dearness Allowance Crosses 50%: The 25% TA Rate Revision (2024 onward)

ch08-004Flag — Scope note: This chapter summarizes the post-2017 Dearness-Allowance-linked rate…
Source L1963–1964

ch08-005

Very High-YieldThe trigger mechanism — why rates rose automatically

O.M.OrderExplanation
Source L1965–1982
Exact-Source Fact Card

The 7th CPC's TA Rules (O.M. 13.07.2017) carry a standing instruction: rates of Mileage Allowance and all Daily Allowance components (hotel, travelling, food) will increase by 25% each time Dearness Allowance rises by 50 percentage points. Dearness Allowance crossed 50% effective 01.01.2024 (confirmed vide DoE, MoF O.M. No.1/1/2024-E.II(B) dated 12.03.2024). Per the precedent set for HRA on 04.08.2021 — where the Department of Expenditure clarified that "this Department's O.M...is very clear and no separate order is being issued" — no fresh dedicated O.M. was necessary to activate the TA/Daily Allowance rate revision either; it took effect automatically by operation of the 2017 O.M.'s own clause. As of 22.11.2026, Dearness Allowance stands at 63% effective 01.07.2026 (DoE O.M. No. 1/2(i)/2026-E.II(B), dated 29.10.2026) — below the next stated 100% trigger. The rate table below reflects the 25% enhancement triggered when Dearness Allowance crossed 50%; verify each underlying rate against the latest applicable order before use.

Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • DA 50% cross hua → Rates automatically 25% badh gaye! Kaise?
  • 2017 ke O.M. mein hi likha tha: Jab 13 July 2017 ko TA Rules ka master O.M. nikla, to usme ek standing clause tha: "Jab bhi DA 50 percentage points badhega (i.e., 50% cross karega, phir 100% cross karega), to Mileage Allowance aur teeno DA components (Hotel/Travel/Food) automatically 25% badh jayenge — koi naya O.M. nikalne ki zaroorat nahi."
  • 1 January 2024 ko trigger hua: DA 50% ho gaya → usi din se saari rates 25% badh gayi. Example: Own car mileage pehle ₹24/km tha → ab ₹30/km. Hotel ceiling Level 9-11 pehle ₹2,250 thi → ab ₹2,812.50. Food Level 9-11 pehle ₹900 tha → ab ₹1,125.
  • Koi naya O.M. nahi nikla! Ye sabse important point hai. Jaise HRA 2021 mein automatically revise hua tha jab DA 25% cross kiya — same logic yahan apply hui. Sarkar ne kaha "humara O.M. clear hai, alag se order nahi chahiye." Exam mein ye bohot puchha jaata hai: "Was a separate O.M. required?" → Answer: NO!
  • Current status (updated 22 November 2026): DA 63% hai, effective 01.07.2026. The previously stated 60% / 01.01.2026 figure was stale. Check current orders; the 25%-enhanced rates in this chapter trace to the earlier 50% trigger.
Memory Hook

"DA 50 → rates +25%, automatically — no separate order, just like HRA in 2021."

Exam Angle / PYQ

Very high-probability question: "Was a separate O.M. required to enhance TA/DA rates when DA crossed 50%?" — Answer: No. This is the single most-tested nuance in this chapter; examiners try to trick candidates into thinking a specific 2024 TA O.M. exists (it doesn't — the mechanism is self-executing).

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

Standing clause of O.M. 13.07.2017: mileage and all three DA components rise 25% each time DA rises 50 points. DA crossed 50% on 01.01.2024 (O.M. 1/1/2024-E.II(B), 12.03.2024). No fresh dedicated TA O.M. needed (HRA precedent 04.08.2021).

Study aid · Memory hook

“DA 50 → rates +25%, automatically — no separate order, just like HRA in 2021.” (source hook)

ch08-006

Fact/Date/Number HeavyCurrent (DA≥50%) Daily Allowance rates — Hotel / Travel / Food, by Pay Level

Source L1983–2004
Exact-Source Fact Card

The table below shows the 2017 base rate against the 25%-enhanced rate reproduced in the UT Ladakh Finance Department's order (No.131-F of 2024, dated 01.05.2024). These figures have not all been independently revalidated in the 22.11.2026 update; check the applicable current order before use.

Pay LevelHotel Ceiling (Current)Travel-within-city Ceiling (Current)Food Lump Sum (Current)
14 and above₹9,375AC taxi actual expenditure₹1,500
12 & 13₹5,625AC taxi up to 50 km/day₹1,250
9 to 11₹2,812.50₹422.50/day₹1,125
6 to 8₹937.50₹281.25/day₹1,000
5 and below₹562.50₹141.25/day₹625
Worked Example

Scenario: A Level 9 officer (Delhi HQ) tours to Mumbai for 2 nights, staying at a hotel actually charging ₹2,600/night (voucher produced), takes local taxis totalling ₹400/day for 3 days, and is away >12 hours each day.
Step 1 — Hotel: Ceiling for Level 9-11 (current) = ₹2,812.50/night. Actual ₹2,600 < ceiling → reimbursed ₹2,600 × 2 = ₹5,200 (voucher-based, actual expenditure within ceiling).
Step 2 — Travel-within-city: Ceiling (current) = ₹422.50/day. Actual ₹400 < ceiling → reimbursed ₹400 × 3 = ₹1,200.
Step 3 — Food (lump sum): ₹1,125/day (current) × 100% (each day >12 hrs away) × 3 days = ₹3,375.
Result: Total DA on tour = ₹5,200 + ₹1,200 + ₹3,375 = ₹9,775 (plus separately claimable air/rail fare).

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

25%-enhanced DA table (reproduced from UT Ladakh order 131-F of 2024 dated 01.05.2024; not all revalidated): L14+ ₹9,375/AC taxi actual/₹1,500; L12–13 ₹5,625/AC taxi ≤50 km/₹1,250; L9–11 ₹2,812.50/₹422.50/₹1,125; L6–8 ₹937.50/₹281.25/₹1,000; ≤L5 ₹562.50/₹141.25/₹625.

Chapter 9 — Worked Illustrations & TA-Bill Practice · source lines 2060–2125

Chapter 9

Worked Illustrations & TA-Bill Practice

ch09-004

High-YieldIllustration 1 — Composite Transfer Grant calculation

Source L2072–2083
Worked Example

Scenario: A Level 10 officer with last basic pay ₹56,100 is transferred from Chennai to Coimbatore (over 400 km apart).
Step 1: Distance ≥ 20 km → CTG applicable.
Step 2: Destination is not A&N/Lakshadweep → standard 80% rate applies.
Step 3: CTG = 80% × ₹56,100 = ₹44,880.
Result: ₹44,880 payable as CTG, in addition to travel fare and personal-effects transportation cost.
Counter-example: If the same officer were transferred instead from Chennai HQ to a suburban office 12 km away, CTG would be nil — only actual conveyance charges (if any) would be considered.

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

Illustration 1: ₹56,100 basic, ≥20 km transfer, 80% → ₹44,880 CTG (plus fare and personal effects). 12 km → CTG nil per illustration.

ch09-005

High-YieldIllustration 2 — Mixed-mode Mileage Allowance

Source L2084–2094
Worked Example

Scenario: A Level 4 official travels 15 km by auto-rickshaw (no state rate notified) then walks the last 2 km to a remote field office.
Step 1: Auto-rickshaw leg: 15 km × current rate ₹15/km = ₹225.
Step 2: On-foot leg: 2 km × current rate ₹15/km = ₹30.
Step 3: Total Mileage Allowance = ₹225 + ₹30 = ₹255.
Takeaway: Mixed-mode journeys are billed leg-by-leg at each mode's applicable rate — they are not averaged.

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

Illustration 2: mixed-mode mileage billed leg-by-leg — 15 km auto ×₹15 = ₹225 + 2 km foot ×₹15 = ₹30 = ₹255.

ch09-006

High-YieldIllustration 3 — Short tour, DA timing-band edge case

Source L2095–2105
Worked Example

Scenario: A Level 13 officer leaves HQ at 8:00 AM and returns the same day at 1:45 PM (5 hours 45 minutes away), on a same-day tour with no hotel stay.
Step 1: Duration = 5h45m → falls in the "<6 hours" band.
Step 2: Applicable % = 30% of the lump-sum food component. Current Level-12/13 food rate = ₹1,250/day.
Step 3: DA = 30% × ₹1,250 = ₹375.
Counter-example: Had the officer returned 20 minutes later (6h05m), the absence would cross into the "6–12 hours" band, paying 70% × ₹1,250 = ₹875 — more than double, for 20 extra minutes. This edge-case is a favourite in numerical MCQs.

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

Illustration 3: 5 h 45 m, Level 13 food ₹1,250 → 30% = ₹375; 6 h 05 m → 70% = ₹875.

ch10-004

Very High-YieldKey definitions — Headquarters, Tour, Family (which dependents qualify)

ProvisoExplanation
Source L2138–2180
Exact-Source Fact Card

Under Supplementary Rules (SR) and DoPT/DoE guidelines, key definitions for Travelling Allowance (TA) purposes are strictly defined:

  • Headquarters (SR 8, SR 116): The station declared to be such for a Government servant by the competent authority (usually where their office is located). TA on tour/transfer is reckoned from this notified station. The signed 12.09.2018 DoE letter uses a beyond-8-km test from the normal place of duty for the stated local-journey TA, irrespective of journey origin; it does not settle every fare/vehicle claim at or below that distance.
  • Tour (SR 61): A journey performed on public duty away from headquarters under proper sanction/authorization by a competent authority. A tour begins when the employee leaves headquarters and ends when they return.
  • Family (SR 2(8)): For TA purposes, "family" comprises the following members who are residing with and wholly dependent on the Government servant:
    • Spouse: Wife or husband of the Government servant. Note that only one spouse is recognized for TA claims. If both husband and wife are Government servants and are transferred from the same old station to the same new station within 6 months, only one can claim TA for the family, and the other will be treated as a dependent family member.
    • Children: Legitimate children and step-children.
      • Sons: Up to the age of 25 years, or until they get married, or until they start earning, whichever is earliest. However, for sons with physical or mental disabilities, there is no age limit, provided they are wholly dependent.
      • Daughters: Until they get married or start earning, whichever is earlier, regardless of age.
      • Divorced/Abandoned/Widowed Daughters: Residing with and wholly dependent, regardless of age.
    • Parents: Dependent parents (including step-parents) residing with the Government servant.
    • Brothers: Dependent minor brothers (under 18 years) residing with the Government servant.
    • Sisters: Dependent unmarried, widowed, or divorced sisters residing with the Government servant, regardless of age.

Dependency Income Ceiling: A family member (other than the spouse) is deemed wholly dependent if their total monthly income from all sources (including pension, temporary increase in pension, and other earnings) does not exceed ₹9,000/- per month plus Dearness Relief (DR) thereon (as per latest 7th CPC standards). If the income exceeds this ceiling, they do not qualify as "family" for TA/LTC/Medical reimbursement.

Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Headquarters kya hai? Wo station jo aapka office officially aapka "base" notify karta hai. Signed 12.09.2018 letter ke mutabik qualifying local-journey threshold normal place of duty se 8 km se zyada hai, chahe aap residence se travel karo. Isse at/below-8-km fares ya vehicle rules decide nahi hote; full current SR 71/GIO abhi open hai.
  • Tour kya hai? Office ke proper sanction/authorization se public duty ke liye HQ se bahar jaana. Departure se return tak tour chalta hai. Bina sanction ke gaye to koi TA nahi milega — chahe kitna bhi zaroori kaam kyun na ho.
  • Family — kaun-kaun shamil hai? Ye bohot detailed definition hai TA/LTC ke liye:
    • Spouse (Husband/Wife): Hamesha dependent maana jaata hai — koi income limit nahi. Lekin sirf ek spouse recognize hota hai. Agar dono govt servants hain, to dono alag-alag claim kar sakte hain (separate rules).
    • Sons (Bete): 25 saal ki age tak ya shaadi tak ya earning tak — jo sabse pehle aaye. Lekin disabled sons ke liye koi age limit nahi — hamesha cover rahenge. Example: 30 saal ka disabled beta jo aapke saath rehta hai aur dependent hai — wo family mein count hoga.
    • Daughters (Betiyaan): Jab tak unmarried hain aur earning nahi — koi age limit nahi! 35 saal ki unmarried beti bhi family mein count hogi. Divorced/widowed/abandoned beti bhi cover hai.
    • Parents: Dependent parents (step-parents bhi) — income ₹9,000/month + DR se kam honi chahiye.
    • Brothers: Sirf minor (18 saal se kam) bhai — dependent aur saath rehne wale.
    • Sisters: Unmarried, widowed, ya divorced behenein — koi age limit nahi — dependent aur saath rehne wali.
  • Dependency Income Ceiling — ₹9,000 + DR: Spouse ko chhod ke, baaki sab family members ki monthly income ₹9,000 + Dearness Relief (DR) se kam honi chahiye. Agar kisi parent ki pension ₹10,000/month hai (DR mila ke), to wo "family" mein nahi aayenge — unka TA claim nahi hoga. Exam mein ye ₹9,000 figure bohot test hota hai — purana 6th CPC figure ₹3,500 tha, ab 7th CPC mein ₹9,000+DR hai.
Memory Hook

"Son-25-Cutoff: Sons leave the TA nest at 25, daughters only upon marriage or earning. Parents are family only if their pocket is under ₹9,000+DR."

Exam Angle / PYQ

Examiners frequently design questions around the dependency age and income limits. Watch out for the ₹9,000/- plus DR ceiling — questions often omit the "plus DR" or use outdated 6th CPC figures (₹3,500). Also, remember that a disabled son has no age limit, which is a common MCQ trap.

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

HQ = station notified by competent authority (SR 8, 116). Tour (SR 61) = public-duty journey away from HQ under sanction. Family (SR 2(8)): one spouse only; sons up to 25 (no limit if disabled and wholly dependent); daughters till marriage/earning; divorced/abandoned/widowed daughters; dependent parents; minor brothers <18; unmarried/widowed/divorced sisters. Dependency ceiling ₹9,000/month + DR.

Study aid · Memory hook

“Son-25-Cutoff.” (source hook)

ch10-005

High-YieldCity classification for HRA & TA (The Actual X/Y/Z List)

ExceptionExplanation
Source L2181–2227
Exact-Source Fact Card

Under the 7th CPC, Indian cities are classified into X, Y and Z tiers based on population criteria. TA hotel ceilings are determined by Pay Level, not by city tier. The 13.07.2017 O.M. separately states a voucher-backed ₹1,000/day hotel ceiling for employees up to Level 8 staying in Class-X cities. Its interaction with the general self-certification route for claims within the ordinary ceiling is unresolved in this guide; do not treat a universal Class-X voucher rule as settled.

  • Class X (8 Cities - Population > 50 Lakh):
    These are the 8 mega-metropolises of India: (1) Delhi (UA, including NCR), (2) Mumbai (Greater Mumbai UA), (3) Kolkata (UA), (4) Chennai (UA), (5) Bengaluru (UA), (6) Hyderabad (UA), (7) Ahmedabad (UA), and (8) Pune (UA).
  • Class Y (approx. 97 Cities - Population 5 Lakh to 50 Lakh):
    Major urban centers and state capitals. Prominent examples include:
    • Andhra Pradesh: Visakhapatnam, Vijayawada, Guntur, Nellore, Kurnool.
    • Bihar: Patna.
    • Chhattisgarh: Raipur, Durg-Bhilai, Bilaspur.
    • Gujarat: Surat, Vadodara, Rajkot, Bhavnagar, Jamnagar.
    • Haryana: Faridabad, Gurugram.
    • Jammu & Kashmir: Srinagar, Jammu.
    • Jharkhand: Ranchi, Dhanbad, Jamshedpur, Bokaro Steel City.
    • Karnataka: Mysuru, Mangaluru, Belagavi, Hubli-Dharwad, Kalaburagi.
    • Kerala: Kochi, Thiruvananthapuram, Kozhikode, Thrissur, Kannur, Kollam.
    • Madhya Pradesh: Bhopal, Indore, Jabalpur, Gwalior, Ujjain.
    • Maharashtra: Nagpur, Nashik, Aurangabad, Solapur, Amravati, Kolhapur, Sangli.
    • Odisha: Bhubaneswar, Cuttack.
    • Punjab: Ludhiana, Amritsar, Jalandhar, Patiala.
    • Rajasthan: Jaipur, Jodhpur, Kota, Bikaner, Ajmer.
    • Tamil Nadu: Coimbatore, Madurai, Tiruchirappalli, Salem, Tiruppur, Erode, Vellore.
    • Uttar Pradesh: Lucknow, Kanpur, Ghaziabad, Noida, Varanasi, Prayagraj, Agra, Meerut, Bareilly, Aligarh, Moradabad, Gorakhpur, Jhansi.
    • Uttarakhand: Dehradun.
    • West Bengal: Asansol, Siliguri, Durgapur.
    • Union Territories: Chandigarh, Puducherry.
  • Class Z (All Remaining Stations - Population < 5 Lakh):
    All other unclassified towns, small districts, and rural areas (e.g. Tokūru or typical sub-divisional postings).
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • X/Y/Z city classification — TA mein iska kya role hai? This classification is primarily used for HRA. The 2017 TA O.M. separately provides a voucher-backed ₹1,000/day hotel ceiling in Class-X cities for Levels 8 and below; whether that also requires vouchers for claims within the ordinary ceiling is unresolved here.
  • 8 Class-X Cities: Delhi (UA+NCR), Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Ahmedabad and Pune. The 2017 O.M. identifies a voucher-backed ₹1,000/day hotel ceiling for Levels 8 and below in Class-X cities; do not infer the unresolved overlap for claims within the ordinary ceiling.
  • ~97 Class-Y Cities: Population 5-50 lakh — jaise Patna, Jaipur, Lucknow, Kochi, Indore, Coimbatore, etc. In mein Level 8- wale bina voucher ke self-certification pe hotel claim kar sakte hain.
  • Class-Z Cities: Baaki sab — population <5 lakh. Yahan bhi self-certification chalta hai.
  • Important: TA hotel/food/travel ceilings are based on Pay Level, not the X/Y/Z tier. The Class-X hotel ceiling is a separate documentation point; its overlap with the general self-certification route remains unresolved in this guide. Do not use city classification to change the ordinary Pay-Level entitlement.
RED-FLAG TRAP

Flag: "TA Daily Allowance rates are determined by city tiers X/Y/Z."
Fix: Under the 7th CPC, the Hotel, Food and Local Travel ceilings are determined by Pay Level, not the X/Y/Z tier. The Class-X hotel clause is a separate voucher-backed ceiling for employees up to Level 8; its overlap with self-certification within the ordinary ceiling is unresolved here. Fix-phrase: "TA ceilings follow Pay Level; check the separate Class-X voucher clause without assuming its unresolved overlap."

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

City tiers: Class X = 8 cities (Delhi, Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Ahmedabad, Pune); Y ≈ 97 cities (5–50 lakh); Z = rest. TA ceilings follow PAY LEVEL, not city tier. Separate voucher-backed ₹1,000/day Class-X hotel ceiling for Level ≤8 — overlap with self-certification unresolved.

Official-text check · read from the official PDF on 26.11.2026

Official Class-X wording: E15.

ch10-006

Moderate-YieldSea/river steamer travel entitlements (Andaman & Nicobar, Lakshadweep)

Explanation
Source L2228–2258
Exact-Source Fact Card

Journeys performed by sea or river steamer are regulated under SR 58 and SR 107. The entitlement is graded strictly by Pay Matrix Level (PML) under the 7th CPC:

Class/Location TypePay Matrix Level (PML)Steamer Entitlement Class
General Journeys
(Other than A&N and Lakshadweep Islands)		PML 9 and aboveHighest Class
PML 6 to 8Lower Class (if there be only two classes) or Middle Class (if there be three classes)
PML 4 & 5Lower Class (if two classes), Middle/Second Class (if three classes), or Third Class (if four classes)
PML 1 to 3Lowest Class
Island Journeys
(Between Mainland and Andaman & Nicobar / Lakshadweep Group of Islands by ships operated by the Shipping Corporation of India Ltd.)		PML 9 and aboveDeluxe Class
PML 6 to 8First Class / 'A' Cabin Class
PML 4 & 5Second Class / 'B' Cabin Class
PML 1 to 3Bunk Class
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Steamer (Ship/Boat) travel — kab use hota hai? Jab aapko Andaman & Nicobar ya Lakshadweep jaana ho ya kisi river/sea journey pe jaana ho — to ship/steamer se travel karna padta hai. In journeys ke liye bhi class entitlements hain Pay Level ke basis pe.
  • General Steamer (Normal rivers/seas): Level 9+ = Highest Class, Level 6-8 = Lower/Middle Class, Level 4-5 = Middle/Third Class, Level 1-3 = Lowest Class. Simple — jitna senior, utna achha cabin.
  • SCI Island Ships (Mainland → A&N/Lakshadweep): Shipping Corporation of India ke ships mein clear classifications hain:
    • Level 9+ = Deluxe Class (sabse achha — private cabin, AC, attached bath)
    • Level 6-8 = First Class / 'A' Cabin
    • Level 4-5 = Second Class / 'B' Cabin
    • Level 1-3 = Bunk Class (dormitory style — shared bunks)
    Yaad rakho: "9+ Deluxe, 6-8 A, 4-5 B, 1-3 Bunk" — ye sequence exam mein puchha ja sakta hai.
Memory Hook

"SCI Ships Cabin Crew: 9+ gets Deluxe, 6-8 gets 'A', 4-5 gets 'B', 1-3 sleeps in Bunks."

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

Steamer (SR 58, 107): general journeys — PML 9+ Highest; PML 6–8 Lower (2 classes)/Middle (3); PML 4–5 Lower/Middle-Second/Third; PML 1–3 Lowest. SCI island ships (mainland ↔ A&N/Lakshadweep): PML 9+ Deluxe; 6–8 First/‘A’ Cabin; 4–5 Second/‘B’; 1–3 Bunk.

Study aid · Memory hook

“SCI Ships Cabin Crew: 9+ Deluxe, 6–8 ‘A’, 4–5 ‘B’, 1–3 Bunk.” (source hook)

ch10-007

Very High-YieldAuthorized travel agents (ATAs) & scope of the July 2026 LTC reminder

O.M.OrderProvisoExceptionExplanation
Source L2259–2287
Exact-Source Fact Card

The Department of Expenditure O.M. No. 19024/03/2021-E.IV dated 16.06.2022 governs Government-account air-ticket booking; DoPT O.M. No. 31011/12/2022-Estt.A-IV dated 29.08.2022 gives the LTC-air instructions. A Government-hosted DGT reproduction of DoPT Cash Section Circular F.No. G-14019/02/2026-Cash dated 16.07.2026 was retrieved. It is addressed to DoPT officers/officials and concerns LTC air booking and LTC advance only. It reiterates the 21-day booking encouragement (not a compulsory claim deadline) and the at-least-30-day advance request, with the specified authorized-travel-agency webpage printout showing suitable flight/fare details, for air-entitled employees under the 29.08.2022 instructions. A direct DoPT-hosted copy was not obtained in this pass; do not extend this reminder to general TA or rail bookings.

  • Air-ticket channels: Under the cited Government-account air-travel orders, use the three Authorized Travel Agents (ATAs) for air tickets within scope. This is not a blanket ATA-only rule for every rail booking. For LTC rail, follow the separate CCS (LTC) rules; DoPT’s 2025 FAQ allows eligible e-ticketing through the Indian Railways website.
    • 1. M/s Balmer Lawrie & Company Limited (BLCL) (govemp.balmerlawrietravelapp.com)
    • 2. M/s Ashok Travels & Tours (ATT) / ITDC (www.attitdc.in)
    • 3. Indian Railways Catering and Tourism Corporation Ltd. (IRCTC) (www.air.irctc.co.in)
  • Correctly scoped timing: DoPT O.M. 29.08.2022 and the 2025 FAQ support the LTC-air 21-day booking encouragement (not a compulsory claim deadline); the O.M. also sets an at-least-30-day LTC-advance request for employees entitled to air travel, with the specified authorized-travel-agency webpage printout showing suitable flight/fare details. The DGT-hosted 16.07.2026 DoPT circular reproduces those instructions for DoPT officers/officials; it does not expand them to general TA or rail. DoE O.M. 16.06.2022 concerns Government-account air-ticket channels/fees, not the LTC 21-day lead.
  • Air India / AITTSL: The airline may be selected as the carrier, but that does not authorize booking directly on an airline website. Under the cited 2022 Government-account instructions, air tickets are booked through the three ATAs; AITTSL is not an ATA. Any departure from the prescribed channel requires a relaxation from the competent authority under the applicable order.
  • Non-Compliance Consequence: A Government-account air ticket booked outside the prescribed ATA channel may be disallowed unless an authorized relaxation is granted under the applicable order for unavoidable circumstances. The 29.08.2022 order identifies the competent authority for its scope. This is an air-ticket rule, not a blanket rail-ticket rule; do not describe it as automatic “100% forfeiture” in every case.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Air-ticket channel: Government-account air tickets, including LTC air tickets within scope, use the three ATAs — IRCTC, Balmer Lawrie, and Ashok Travels (ATT). This is not a blanket rule that every rail ticket must be booked through an ATA; LTC rail has separate rules.
  • AITTSL / channel: Air India’s former travel-services entity is not one of the prescribed ATAs. For air tickets within scope, use the required ATA channel or obtain an authorized relaxation; do not infer a rail-booking rule.
  • Air-ticket channel: If a Government-account air ticket is booked outside the prescribed ATA channel, the fare may be disallowed unless the applicable order permits an authorized relaxation for unavoidable circumstances. Do not apply this to every rail ticket.
  • Channel caution: Airline choice is not the same as permission to book directly on the airline website. Use the required ATA channel for air tickets under the cited orders; seek an authorized relaxation before using another channel.
  • Best Available Fare: Compare the fare in the order’s fixed three-hour band, preferably non-stop, and retain the ATA webpage printout. For LTC, the 21-day lead time is encouraged—not a compulsory deadline.
Memory Hook

"I-B-A = air-booking agents: IRCTC, Balmer Lawrie, Ashok Travels. Use the prescribed ATA channel for air tickets within scope; this mnemonic is not a rail-booking rule."

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

Air tickets on Government account: three ATAs (BLCL, ATT/ITDC, IRCTC); DoE O.M. 16.06.2022; LTC-air O.M. 29.08.2022: 21-day booking = encouragement; LTC-advance request ≥30 days for air-entitled. 16.07.2026 DGT-hosted circular: DoPT officers/officials, LTC air only. Not a blanket rail rule; outside-ATA air ticket may be disallowed unless relaxation.

Study aid · Memory hook

“I-B-A = IRCTC, Balmer Lawrie, Ashok Travels” — air only, not a rail rule. (source hook)

ch10-008

Very High-YieldThe Crucial LTC vs. TA/DA Entitlement Contrasts (High-Yield Exam Distinctions)

O.M.ProvisoExceptionExplanation
Source L2288–2345
Exact-Source Fact Card

Under DoPT and Department of Expenditure circulars (clarified via DoPT O.M. F. No. 31011/8/2017-Estt.A-IV dated 19th September 2017 and O.M. dated 04.02.2021), the entitlements for Leave Travel Concession (LTC) and Travelling Allowance (TA) on Tour/Transfer exhibit critical, heavily tested differences:

  • 1. Air Entitlement Difference for Pay Levels 6 to 8 (Classic Exam Trap):
    • On Tour/Training/Transfer: Government servants in Pay Levels 6 to 8 are entitled to travel by Air (Economy Class) within India.
    • On LTC: Government servants in Pay Levels 6 to 8 are NOT entitled to travel by Air! Their normal LTC entitlement is restricted to rail as specified in CCS (LTC) Rule 12, subject to any current special-dispensation or later order.
    • Exception: Air travel is allowed for Levels 6 to 8 only under the "Special Dispensation Scheme" (for travel to Northeast Region, J&K, Ladakh, and Andaman & Nicobar) from designated railheads, or with special relaxation.
  • 2. Daily Allowance (DA) & Incidentals:
    • On Tour: Daily Allowance (Hotel, Food, Local conveyance) is fully admissible under standard ceilings.
    • On LTC: No Daily Allowance (DA) of any kind is admissible. Incidental expenses and local journeys (stay, boarding, taxis within the city) are non-reimbursable under the cited LTC rule.
  • 3. Travel in Private/Hired Vehicles (Own Car / Hired Taxi):
    • On Tour/Transfer: Road mileage is fully reimbursable for journeys performed in own car, hired taxi, or public transport.
    • On LTC: Travel is admissible only in vehicles operated by the Government or Public Sector Corporations (Central/State/Local body), subject to the stated exceptions. Private transport is not admissible except where a specific rule or exception applies.
    • Exceptions on LTC:
      • Disability exception (LTC only): DoPT’s 01.07.2025 LTC FAQ, Q30, says own/hired taxi may be used where disability of the employee or dependent makes authorized modes infeasible, subject to a competent-authority medical certificate, an undertaking that authorized travel is not feasible and the claimant actually used the own/hired vehicle, and a reimbursement cap no higher than entitled rail/air fare by the shortest route. Do not extend this to local official-tour travel.
      • Unconnected Places (DoPT O.M. dated 04.02.2021): If the relevant places are not connected by public transport, the stated private-transport exception is limited to a maximum of 100 km each side, subject to the applicable order and evidence requirements.
  • 4. Tour Packages:
    • On LTC: Tour packages run by private travel operators are not allowed. Only the tour packages permitted by the applicable CCS (LTC) instructions may be claimed; verify the allowed operator and fare component.
    • Reimbursement Scale: Only the travel fare component is reimbursable. The organizing agency must separately indicate and certify the fare component from the boarding/lodging package.
  • 5. Claim Submission Timelines (Contrast with TA):
    • If LTC advance is drawn: The claim for reimbursement is normally due within one month after completion of the return journey (CCS (LTC) Rule 15(vi)). A later, limited relaxation may allow up to three months with FA concurrence if the entire advance plus penal interest is refunded in one lump sum.
    • If no LTC advance is drawn: The claim is normally due within three months after completion of the return journey (Rule 14). A later, limited FA-concurred relaxation may admit it within six months.
    • TA Contrast: The ordinary 60-day TA claim-submission limit applies whether or not a TA advance was drawn; advance adjustment is separate. The inspected 2005 Compendium states 15 days for tour advances, but its currency is unverified and no general 30-day transfer deadline was located.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • LTC vs Tour/Transfer — 5 bade farq jo exams mein aate hain:
  • 1. Level 6-8 Air Ticket: Tour pe → Flight allowed (Economy). LTC pe → Flight NOT allowed (sirf AC-II Tier train)! Exception: Special dispensation scheme under J&K, NER, Ladakh, A&N jaane ke liye. Ye sabse commonly tested difference hai.
  • 2. Daily Allowance: Tour pe → Hotel + Food + Travel DA milta hai. LTC pe → Koi DA nahi — sirf aane-jaane ka ticket fare. LTC personal vacation hai — sarkar khana/hotel nahi degi.
  • 3. Private Taxi: Tour TA and LTC have different rules. For LTC, a disability-related own/hired taxi is only a conditional exception (medical certificate, undertaking, actual journey, fare cap); the public-transport-unconnected route is a separate exception with its own 100 km each-side limit. Neither exception establishes a pay-level-independent local AC taxi claim on official tour/training.
  • 4. Tour Packages: LTC pe sirf ITDC, STDC (State Tourism), ya IRCTC ke tour packages allowed hain. Private tour operators (Thomas Cook, Cox & Kings, etc.) ke packages se LTC claim nahi hoga.
  • 5. Claim Deadlines: LTC advance drawn → the bill is normally due within one month after the return journey; no advance → three months. The DoPT O.M. dated 21.12.2023 provides limited FA-concurred relaxation (up to three months with advance, subject to full refund plus penal interest; up to six months without advance). Ordinary TA claims have a 60-day submission window whether or not an advance was drawn; TA advance adjustment is a separate, not-fully-established question.
Memory Hook

"LTC is Government-Only, Train-Only (for Level 6-8). No DA, no private taxi (unless <100km unconnected), and no private tour packages!"

Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

LTC vs TA: L6–8 air on tour but not on LTC (special dispensation exception); no DA on LTC; LTC private vehicles only per exceptions (disability — FAQ Q30; unconnected places max 100 km each side); no private tour packages; LTC claim periods in months (1/3 months, relaxation 3/6) vs ordinary TA 60 days.

Official-text check · read from the official PDF on 26.11.2026

GFR Rule 292 states 30 / 60 days for LTC claims — conflict with the months-based periods here: E9 [uncertain].

ch10-009

High-YieldCancellation-charge reimbursement rules

ProvisoExceptionExplanation
Source L2346–2369
Exact-Source Fact Card

Under the TA/DA rules, cancellation and rescheduling charges for air/rail/bus tickets booked on Government account are reimbursable subject to strict conditions:

  • Official Exigency: The cancellation or postponement of the official tour, training, or transfer must be necessitated by public/official interest under the order of the competent authority.
  • Supporting Certificates: The claim for reimbursement must be accompanied by:
    • A certificate from the Controlling Officer stating that the cancellation/postponement was due to official exigencies of service.
    • Receipts/vouchers issued by the authorized travel agents (or airlines/railways) indicating the actual cancellation fee or deduction made.
  • Personal Reasons: If the ticket is cancelled due to personal reasons of the employee, the cancellation charges must be borne by the employee themselves, unless specifically condoned under emergency medical circumstances by the Head of Department.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Tour cancel hua to cancellation charges kaun dega? Agar aapne official tour ka ticket book kiya aur phir tour cancel/postpone ho gaya, to cancellation charges reimbursable hain — lekin conditions ke saath:
  • Official reason se cancel hua: Agar public/official interest mein tour cancel hua (boss ne bola "meeting postpone ho gayi" ya "budget nahi hai"), to poora cancellation charge sarkar degi. Conditions: (1) Controlling Officer ka certificate chahiye ki "ye cancellation official exigency mein hua," (2) Airline/railway ka cancellation receipt/proof chahiye.
  • Personal reason se cancel hua: Agar aapne apne personal reason se tour cancel kiya (jaise "meri tabiyat kharab nahi hai, bas man nahi hai"), to cancellation charges aapko khud bharna padega — sarkar nahi degi. Exception: Emergency medical circumstances mein HoD condone kar sakta hai.
Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

Cancellation charges reimbursable only if official exigency (competent authority order), with Controlling Officer certificate and agent/airline/railway receipt; personal-reason cancellations borne by employee unless condoned by HoD in emergency medical circumstances.

ch10-010

High-YieldRules on journeys by a longer / circuitous route

ProvisoExceptionExplanation
Source L2370–2399
Exact-Source Fact Card

Under SR 30 and SR 31, Travelling Allowance must be calculated based on the shortest and cheapest route usually open to the public:

  • Shortest Route Rule (SR 30): The shortest route is that by which the traveler can most speedily reach their destination. If two routes are equally short, but one is cheaper, the cheaper route must be used.
  • Personal Convenience (SR 31): If a Government servant performs a journey by a longer or circuitous route for personal reasons or convenience:
    • Their TA/fare reimbursement is strictly restricted to the fare and road mileage of the shortest route.
    • The excess time spent on travel via the longer route is treated as leave (Casual Leave/Earned Leave) and cannot be treated as duty.
  • Public Interest Exception: A journey by a longer/circuitous route may be allowed with full TA reimbursement and treated as duty ONLY IF:
    • The longer route is specifically prescribed or approved by the competent authority in public interest.
    • The shortest route is blocked or disrupted due to natural disasters (landslides, floods), civil unrest, or suspension of train/road services, and proof of such blockage is furnished.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Ghum ke gaye to kya hoga? (SR 30 & SR 31): Rule simple hai: hamesha shortest route se travel karo. Agar aap apni marzi se lamba rasta (circuitous route) choose karte ho personal convenience ke liye, to 2 consequences hain:
  • 1. Fare restriction: Sarkar sirf shortest route ka fare degi — jo extra paisa aapne lambe raste pe kharcha kiya, wo aapki jeb se jaayega. Example: Delhi-Mumbai shortest route = ₹3,000, lekin aap Delhi-Jaipur-Ahmedabad-Mumbai gaye = ₹5,000. Sarkar sirf ₹3,000 degi — baaki ₹2,000 aapka personal kharcha.
  • 2. Extra time = Leave: Jo extra din lambe raste pe lage — wo duty nahi, leave maane jayenge (CL/EL). Example: Shortest route se 1 din lagta, lekin aapne ghum ke 3 din lagaye — to 2 extra din aapki CL/EL se katenge.
  • Exception — Public Interest: Agar (1) competent authority ne written order diya hai ki "is route se jaao" (public interest mein), ya (2) shortest route blocked hai (floods, landslides, civil unrest, rail roko) aur proof hai — to lambe raste ka full fare + full duty days milenge.
Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

SR 30/31: shortest and cheapest route; longer route for personal convenience → fare/mileage of shortest route, excess time = leave. Exceptions (full TA, duty): route prescribed/approved in public interest; shortest route blocked by disaster/unrest/suspension with proof.

ch10-011

Moderate-YieldDDO & Controlling Officer certificates required on a TA bill

OrderProvisoExplanation
Source L2400–2425
Exact-Source Fact Card

To ensure financial propriety and prevent double billing under Treasury Rules and G.A.R. 14-A (Tour) / G.A.R. 14-B (Transfer), specific certificates must be recorded and signed by the Drawing and Disbursing Officer (DDO) and the Controlling Officer before passing a TA bill:

  • 1. Proper Sanction: "Certified that the journeys/mileage claims made in this bill were performed under proper sanction and strictly in the interest of public service."
  • 2. Class & Fare Verification: "Certified that I have checked the claimant’s travel class and, where the claim includes an air ticket, compliance with the applicable Government-account ATA instructions."
  • 3. Shortest Route: "Certified that the journeys were performed by the shortest and most economical route usually open to the public."
  • 4. No Free Transport: "Certified that the claimant was not provided with free Government transport/conveyance for any part of the journey for which road mileage has been claimed." (Or if provided, the mileage has been proportionately deducted/not claimed).
  • 5. Original Order Endorsement: "Certified that the original tour/transfer/LTC order has been produced and a note of this TA claim has been endorsed thereon to prevent duplicate claims."
  • 6. Scrutiny of Vouchers (Transfer/Retirement): "Certified that the details of actual expenditure for the transportation of personal effects and conveyance have been scrutinized and found to be correct, reasonable, and supported by authentic vouchers."
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • DDO (Drawing & Disbursing Officer) ko TA bill pass karne se pehle kya-kya check karna hai? Ye financial propriety ke 6 certificates hain jo DDO ko sign karne padte hain:
  • 1. Proper Sanction: "Ye journey properly sanctioned thi — boss ne permission di thi — aur public interest mein thi." Bina sanctioned tour ke koi TA nahi.
  • 2. Class & Fare Verification: Verify the admissible class for the journey. If the claim includes an air ticket, check the applicable ATA channel and any authorized relaxation; rail-ticket evidence follows its separate applicable rules.
  • 3. Shortest Route: "Journey shortest route se hui" — ghum ke nahi gaye.
  • 4. No Free Transport: "Employee ko koi free sarkari gaadi nahi mili thi is journey ke liye" — agar mili thi to mileage claim nahi kar sakte.
  • 5. Original Order Endorsement: "Original tour/transfer order pe is TA claim ka note laga diya hai — taaki double billing na ho." Matlab ek hi order pe do baar claim nahi kar sakte.
  • 6. Voucher Scrutiny (Transfer/Retirement): "Baggage transport ke vouchers maine check kiye hain — sahi hain, reasonable hain, authentic hain."
Study aid · Quick revision (not source text)

Six DDO/Controlling Officer certificates: proper sanction; class & fare (ATA compliance for air); shortest route; no free transport for mileage; original order endorsed; vouchers scrutinised for personal effects/conveyance.

ch10-014

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 10

ProvisoException
Source L2444–2455
  • Family includes dependent parents, minor brothers, and unmarried/widowed/divorced sisters if their monthly income is under ₹9,000 + DR.
  • Sons are excluded from TA once they reach 25 years, get married, or earn. Disabled sons have no age limit.
  • 8 Class-X cities are Delhi, Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Ahmedabad and Pune. The 2017 O.M. sets a separate voucher-backed ₹1,000/day hotel ceiling for Levels 8 and below in these cities; the overlap with the ordinary self-certification ceiling is unresolved here.
  • Government-account air tickets use the required ATA channel, subject to authorized relaxation. This rule is about air tickets, not every rail ticket; LTC rail follows separate instructions and eligible rail e-ticketing through Indian Railways is allowed.
  • Steamer travel cabin class for SCI island ships is Deluxe (PML 9+), First/'A' (PML 6-8), Second/'B' (PML 4-5), and Bunk (PML 1-3).
  • Circuitous route journeys are capped at the shortest route fare, and excess time is treated as leave, unless authorized in public interest.

Study aid · Practice MCQs (written for this guide; answers cite the source card)
Q1. Dependency income ceiling for a family member (other than spouse)?
  1. ₹3,500
  2. ₹9,000 plus DR
  3. ₹9,000 flat
  4. ₹25,000

Answer: (b) ₹9,000 plus DR — “does not exceed ₹9,000/- per month plus Dearness Relief (DR)”. The 6th CPC figure ₹3,500 is the trap. Rule citation: ch10-004 (L2138–2180)

Q2. A disabled, wholly dependent son — age limit for TA ‘family’?
  1. 18
  2. 21
  3. 25
  4. No age limit

Answer: (d) No age limit — “for sons with physical or mental disabilities, there is no age limit”. Rule citation: ch10-004

Q3. How many Class-X cities does the guide list?
  1. 4
  2. 6
  3. 8
  4. 97

Answer: (c) 8 — “Class X (8 Cities)”. Rule citation: ch10-005 (L2181–2227)

Q4. On SCI island ships, PML 6–8 officers are entitled to:
  1. Deluxe
  2. First Class / ‘A’ Cabin
  3. Second / ‘B’ Cabin
  4. Bunk

Answer: (b) First Class / ‘A’ Cabin — PML 9+ Deluxe; 6–8 First/‘A’; 4–5 Second/‘B’; 1–3 Bunk. Rule citation: ch10-006 (L2228–2258)

Q5. Pay Levels 6 to 8 on LTC (normal entitlement) as against tour:
  1. Economy air on both
  2. Not entitled to air on LTC; rail under CCS (LTC) Rule 12, subject to later orders/special dispensation
  3. No travel entitlement
  4. AC-I only

Answer: (b) Not entitled to air on LTC; rail under CCS (LTC) Rule 12, subject to later orders/special dispensation — Classic exam trap — tour air yes, LTC air no. Rule citation: ch10-008 (L2288–2345)

Q6. Journey by a longer route for personal convenience — TA admissible?
  1. Full fare
  2. Fare/mileage of shortest route only; excess time treated as leave
  3. Nil
  4. Average of both routes

Answer: (b) Fare/mileage of shortest route only; excess time treated as leave — SR 31. Rule citation: ch10-010 (L2370–2399)

ch11-005

Source-status warningSR 164 training claims — do not apply an unverified universal rule

O.M.OrderProvisoExplanation
Source L2485–2503
Targeted primary/secondary-source review · 22.11.2026

Verification gate: STOP. The current official SR 164/GIO compilation and the original historical orders beneath SR 164 were not retrieved in verifiable primary form. Secondary compilations and reproduced copies identify issues to check, but they do not support a categorical current claim or exam answer. Official DoE O.M. 01.02.2018 is a separate instruction for free boarding/lodging; it does not, by itself, resolve the distinct fixed-rate residential-training allowance.

  • Ordinary training DA schedules: Secondary transcriptions describe full DA for the first 180 days where boarding/lodging are not provided, and first 30 days full plus next 150 days half where institute facilities exist. The current primary text, conditions, and status were not verified.
  • Compulsory fixed-rate residential programmes: A separate historical special allowance is transcribed as actual boarding/lodging expenditure plus one-quarter of full DA for outstation participants, and actual boarding/lodging expenditure only for local participants. A secondary ISTM record cites DoPT O.M. dated 12.01.1988 (reported as No. 14011/5/87-T.N.P.); the signed primary order and its current status were not located. Its interaction with DoE O.M. 01.02.2018 remains open.
  • Courses exceeding 180 days / option: Some secondary compilations report an option between a transfer-TA route and a tour-TA/DA route for qualifying courses. Do not convert that into an automatic transfer classification or assume full family, baggage, conveyance, or CTG entitlements without the governing primary text and sanction. The purported DoP memo dated 21.05.2024 is a secondary-hosted copy; it cites “Decision No. 4 below Rule 164” for a course not exceeding 180 days, and does not resolve the >180-day option. The GIO/Decision labels in secondary sources are not consistent and must not be treated as interchangeable.
  • Theory and practical training at different stations: Secondary transcriptions of P&T orders from 1982/1984 describe a fresh-course treatment at the practical-training station in a limited in-service/refresher setting. The original letters, exact conditions, current status, and applicability to other departments/candidates were not verified. Do not generalize this to a fresh 180-day limit at every station change.
  • Tour during training: A secondary transcription associates a 17.10.1979 order with a tour to another station not terminating the halt at the training station for DA determination. This does not, without the original text, establish a general full-DA entitlement at the tour station or a universal rule for counting days away.
  • Training at headquarters / Urban Agglomeration: Secondary training material describes no TA/DA for training at the headquarters station. The current primary wording was not verified. The separate claim that this extends to the entire Urban Agglomeration is not established; no UA-wide ban is stated here.

Exam and claim use: Do not score a general second-station reset or apply a universal 30/150 schedule. Verify the current primary SR/GIO, relevant department/cadre order, training sanction, and facility/cost facts; refer disputed claims to the competent authority. TA-COV-17 remains pending [D].

Sources: DoE TA O.M., 13.07.2017 · DoE O.M., 01.02.2018 · purported DoP 21.05.2024 memo, secondary reproduction only.

Exam-safe takeaway

Training status is not established by duration or station change alone. Keep ordinary schedules, free boarding/lodging, the reported fixed-rate special allowance, and the limited P&T theory/practical orders separate. No general station-reset answer is verified.
